1. First: What is BusinessIntelligence.ai actually supposed to be?

It should NOT be:

"A website that contains artificially generated sales data and tells you why revenue went down."

That would be only a demonstration of the algorithm.

The better product definition is:

BusinessIntelligence.ai is a business intelligence platform where a business can bring/connect its own data, define or discover its KPIs, and receive evidence-backed explanations, insights, and recommended actions.

The Round 2 prototype can use synthetic/sample data to demonstrate this workflow, because the challenge explicitly says real enterprise data is not expected and that a working prototype on illustrative/sample data is acceptable.

So there are two separate things:

Layer	Purpose
Product vision	Analyze a real company's own business data
Round 2 prototype	Demonstrate that capability using controlled/simulated data
Architecture	Must therefore be designed so simulated data can later be replaced by user data

This is the direction I recommend.

2. The biggest gap I found

Your current plan already has:

CSV
API
Database
Snowflake
PostgreSQL
Databricks

as possible connectors.

It also has:

Data Source Registry
Scheduler
Freshness
Background Jobs

and even describes:

Upload
 ↓
Job created
 ↓
Processing
 ↓
Background ingestion
 ↓
Complete

for large document ingestion.

But there is a missing product layer:
USER
 ↓
"Add my business data"
 ↓
Choose source
 ↓
Upload/connect
 ↓
Preview
 ↓
Detect schema
 ↓
Map columns
 ↓
Identify dimensions/measures
 ↓
Validate data
 ↓
Define KPI / semantic contract
 ↓
Confirm
 ↓
Dataset becomes available
 ↓
Analyze

That complete user-facing data onboarding flow is not clearly specified in the current implementation plan.

The current plan jumps too quickly from:

BUSINESS DATA SOURCES
        ↓
DATA INGESTION
        ↓
SQLite

without adequately defining how the business user gets their source into that first box.

3. How the actual website should be used

I would make the product experience something like this.

Step 1 — User creates/selects a business workspace
BusinessIntelligence.ai

├── KPI Dashboard
├── Investigations

└── Governance
4. Step 2 — User adds their business data

A prominent:

+ Add Data Source

button.

Then:

How do you want to add data?

┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ Upload File                 │
│ CSV / XLSX / JSON           │
└─────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ Connect Database             │
│ PostgreSQL / SQL etc.       │
└─────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ Connect API                 │
│ REST / Business API         │
└─────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ Upload Documents             │
│ PDF / DOCX / TXT etc.       │
└─────────────────────────────┘

For the hackathon, you do not need to actually support every connector.

You can support:

CSV/XLSX + PDF/TXT

and architect the connector interface so APIs/databases can be added later.

5. Step 3 — Upload structured business data

Suppose a company uploads:

sales.csv

Containing:

Date	Product	Region	Channel	Orders	Revenue	Discount
2026-08-01	A	North	Online	120	12000	500
2026-08-01	B	South	Retail	80	9000	200

The system should not immediately throw this into an LLM.

Instead:

Upload
 ↓
File validation
 ↓
Schema inference
 ↓
Data profiling
 ↓
Column classification
 ↓
Quality checks
 ↓
User confirmation
 ↓
Ingestion

This is critical because the problem statement specifically expects data checks, processing, EDA, visualization, and statistical analysis.

6. Step 4 — Automatic schema understanding

The system should identify:

Date       → temporal dimension
Product    → categorical dimension
Region     → categorical dimension
Channel    → categorical dimension
Orders     → measure
Revenue    → measure
Discount   → measure

Then show the user:

We detected 4 dimensions and 3 measures.

Allow correction.

For example:

Revenue
Detected as:
✓ Measure

Aggregation:
SUM

Currency:
INR

Semantic role:
Primary financial KPI candidate
7. Step 5 — Data quality report

Before analysis:

Dataset Health

Rows                  1,240,500
Columns               12

Missing values        1.8%
Duplicate rows        0.2%
Invalid dates         0
Negative revenue     12
Outliers              43

Overall:
✓ Suitable for analysis

This is important because the problem statement explicitly asks teams to document data checks and processing tasks.

Your current plan has data-quality concepts, but the user-facing onboarding/reporting experience should be made explicit.

8. Step 6 — KPI configuration

After ingestion:

What does your business want to monitor?

The system could automatically suggest:

Detected KPI candidates

Revenue
Orders
AOV
Discount Rate
Return Rate

User can select:

✓ Revenue
✓ Orders
✓ AOV

Then the semantic contract becomes:

KPI: Revenue

Formula:
SUM(revenue)

Dimensions:
region
product
channel
date

Drivers:
orders
AOV
discount
stockouts

Threshold:
5%

Owner:
Sales

This fits extremely well with the problem statement's requirement for a lightweight KPI/semantic contract covering definitions, calculations, drivers, thresholds, lineage and access restrictions.

That is an internal architecture, not the product UX.

11. There are actually TWO kinds of user data

This is something your architecture should explicitly distinguish.

Type A — Structured business data

Examples:

sales.csv
orders.xlsx
inventory.csv
marketing.csv
finance database
ERP
CRM

Used mainly by:

M1 → M2 → M3

and partially by M4 for structured evidence.

Type B — Unstructured/contextual evidence

Examples:

inventory_report.pdf
management_report.pdf
customer_complaints.pdf
operations_report.docx
emails.txt
support tickets

Used mainly by:

M4 → M5

Your current plan already has a strong design for this. Module 4 separates structured evidence from unstructured evidence and provides ingestion, chunking, embeddings, retrieval and evidence scoring.

Your existing Module 4 architecture already handles this conceptually.

13. What if the company's datasets have completely different schemas?

This is the important part.

Suppose Company A has:

sales_date
net_sales
cust_id
territory

Company B has:

transaction_date
revenue
customer
region

TRACE should not require both companies to use the same column names.

Instead:

Company dataset
       ↓
Schema Mapper
       ↓
Canonical business model
       ↓
KPI Semantic Contract
       ↓
Analysis

For example:

net_sales       ─┐
revenue         ─┼──→ revenue
sales_amount    ─┘

territory       ─┐
region          ─┼──→ region
sales_region    ─┘

This is one of the most important additions I would make to the implementation plan.

16. This does NOT mean you need another Module 8

I strongly recommend not creating another module just for data upload.

Instead, expand Module 1.

Module 1 should become:

Data Foundation, Onboarding & KPI Semantic Layer

rather than simply:

KPI Foundation

Its responsibilities become:

M1
│
├── Dataset onboarding
├── Schema inference
├── Column mapping
├── Data validation
├── Data profiling
├── Normalization
├── Data quality
├── Data freshness metadata
├── Canonical business model
├── KPI semantic contract
├── KPI calculation
├── KPI history
├── KPI breakdown
└── KPI lineage

Then Module 7 handles the operational mechanism that keeps those datasets refreshed.

That gives a very clean separation:

M1 = WHAT IS THE DATA?

M7 = HOW DOES DATA GET INTO / STAY IN THE SYSTEM?
17. Very important distinction: M1 vs M7 for data upload

Don't put all ingestion into M7.

M1 owns the meaning:
What is this column?
What is a metric?
What is a dimension?
What does revenue mean?
How should it aggregate?
What is the grain?
M7 owns the operation:
When should it refresh?
Did the source respond?
Did ingestion fail?
Should we retry?
Is the source stale?
Should this job run in background?

So:

                  DATA SOURCE
                       │
                       ▼
              ┌─────────────────┐
              │       M7        │
              │ ingestion ops   │
              │ scheduling      │
              │ retry           │
              │ freshness       │
              └────────┬────────┘
                       │
                       ▼
              ┌─────────────────┐
              │       M1        │
              │ schema          │
              │ validation      │
              │ normalization   │
              │ semantics       │
              │ KPI             │
              └────────┬────────┘
                       │
                       ▼
                    M2 → M3
18. What about artificial data?

This is where the current plan is slightly misleading.

Your plan currently talks heavily about:

generateData.js
synthetic evidence
controlled scenarios
synthetic data
evidence.json

That's fine for the prototype.

In fact, the problem statement explicitly permits simulated data and even requires a multi-factor KPI movement with known or simulated underlying drivers.

But the architecture should say:

                 DATA SOURCE
                     │
          ┌──────────┴──────────┐
          │                     │
     REAL USER DATA       SYNTHETIC DATA
          │                     │
          └──────────┬──────────┘
                     ▼
              SAME M1 PIPELINE
                     ▼
                    M2
                     ▼
                    M3
                     ▼
                    M4
                     ▼
                    M5
                     ▼
                    M6
                     ▼
                    M7

That is the right architecture.

Synthetic data is therefore a data source implementation, not the definition of the product.

20. A user journey should look like this

Imagine an actual company.

User logs in
Welcome to BusinessIntelligence.ai
Adds data
+ Add Data

sales.xlsx
marketing.csv
operations.csv
TRACE analyzes the datasets
sales.xlsx
✓ 1.2M rows
✓ 14 columns
✓ Date detected
✓ Revenue detected
✓ Product detected
✓ Region detected

Data quality: 97%
User confirms mappings
Revenue → revenue
Region → region
Product → product
Date → date
TRACE creates KPIs
Revenue
Orders
AOV
Return Rate
Dashboard appears
Revenue
₹9.12M
↓ 8.8%

Orders
96K
↓ 4%

AOV
₹95
↓ 4.9%
User clicks Revenue

Why did revenue decline?

Then:

M2
Revenue movement is material
        ↓
M3
Orders + AOV + stockouts identified
        ↓
M4
Inventory + operations + support evidence
        ↓
M5
Narrative + recommendation

Final answer:

Revenue declined 8.8%, primarily associated with a 4% reduction in orders and a 4.9% reduction in AOV. Stockout events in the North region explain an estimated 31% of the decline. Inventory and support-ticket evidence support the stockout hypothesis, while pricing evidence contradicts a major pricing-driven explanation.

Then:

Recommended action: Prioritize replenishment for Products A and B in North.

That's BusinessIntelligence.ai.

21. What the judges should see

This is especially important given the Round 2 requirements.

The judges don't need to see:

"Here is our CSV parser."

They should see:

1. Upload/connect data
        ↓
2. TRACE understands it
        ↓
3. KPI dashboard
        ↓
4. Detects important movement
        ↓
5. Finds drivers
        ↓
6. Retrieves evidence
        ↓
7. Explains uncertainty
        ↓
8. Recommends action
        ↓
9. User accepts/challenges
        ↓
10. Governance + telemetry

That demonstrates essentially the entire problem statement.

22. What I recommend changing in implementation_plan.txt

I would make four major changes.

Change 1 — Add a new section before Module 1
0. USER DATA ONBOARDING & PRODUCT WORKFLOW

Define:

Upload/connect
→ Preview
→ Schema detection
→ Mapping
→ Validation
→ Normalization
→ KPI configuration
→ Analysis
Change 2 — Expand Module 1

Rename:

Module 1 — Data Foundation & KPI Semantic Layer

to:

Module 1 — Data Onboarding, Governed Data Foundation & KPI Semantic Layer

Add:

Dataset registry
Schema inference
Column mapping
Data profiling
Data validation
Normalization
Dataset versioning
Canonical business model
KPI semantic contract
Change 3 — Explicitly define supported data classes
Structured:
CSV
XLSX
JSON
SQL
API

Unstructured:
PDF
DOCX
TXT
support tickets
reports
reviews

And specify which module handles each.

Change 4 — Make synthetic data explicitly a prototype adapter

Instead of:

TRACE uses synthetic data

write:

Synthetic data is provided as a controlled prototype source for demonstrating the intelligence pipeline. It must implement the same governed data contracts used by user-provided datasets, so that replacing synthetic data with real business data does not require changes to Modules 2–6.

That is a much stronger architectural statement.

23. Final answer to your central question
Is BusinessIntelligence.ai supposed to upload business data and analyze it?

Yes — that should be the product vision.

Is the Round 2 prototype allowed to use artificial data?

Yes. Absolutely. The challenge explicitly allows illustrative/sample/simulated data and does not expect access to proprietary enterprise data.

Does your current implementation plan make that distinction clearly enough?

No.

It currently has a strong data-processing backend architecture and strong synthetic-data prototype architecture, but the user-facing onboarding of arbitrary business datasets is under-specified.

Is this a fatal problem?

No. It is relatively easy to fix architecturally, and I would fix it now before more implementation is done.

The key principle

The user's data enters through M1/M7, but after normalization the same intelligence pipeline works regardless of whether the underlying data was synthetic, uploaded as CSV, imported from Excel, queried from a database, or obtained through an API.

That is the architecture that makes the project a real BusinessIntelligence.ai product, rather than a demo built around artificial data.

And it aligns much better with the actual Round 2 objective: heterogeneous sources, different grains and refresh cadences, governed KPI semantics, evidence-backed explanations, persona-specific recommendations, uncertainty, feedback, security and operational constraints.














TRACE — MODULE 1 IMPLEMENTATION PLAN
Governed Data Foundation, Data Onboarding & KPI Semantic Layer

Status: Authoritative implementation specification

Module: Module 1

Primary responsibility: Data ingestion/onboarding, governed structured data, schema understanding, data quality, KPI semantics, deterministic KPI computation, historical KPI observations, freshness, and lineage.

0. Critical Product Principle

BusinessIntelligence.ai is not a synthetic-data-only application.

The product must support this user journey:

Business User
      │
      ▼
Add Business Data
      │
      ├── Upload CSV
      ├── Upload XLSX
      ├── Upload JSON
      ├── Connect Database
      └── Connect API
      │
      ▼
Data Onboarding
      │
      ├── Schema Detection
      ├── Column/type Detection
      ├── Data Profiling
      ├── Data Quality
      ├── Column Mapping
      ├── Grain Detection
      └── User Confirmation
      │
      ▼
Governed Data Layer
      │
      ▼
KPI Semantic Layer
      │
      ▼
Deterministic KPI Calculation
      │
      ▼
KPI API
      │
      ├── Module 2
      ├── Module 3
      ├── Module 4
      ├── Module 5
      └── Decision Workspace

Synthetic data is only one possible data source.

For the Round 2 prototype, synthetic data is required/encouraged because the challenge explicitly permits simulated or illustrative data and asks for a controlled multi-factor KPI scenario.

The architecture must therefore allow:

Synthetic Data ─────┐
                    │
User Uploaded Data ─┼──→ Same Module 1 Pipeline
                    │
Connected Sources ──┘
1. Module 1 Objective

Module 1 is the quantitative source of truth for TRACE.

Its job is to take business data from available source systems, onboard and understand that data, store it in a governed relational structure, define what every KPI means, calculate KPIs deterministically, and expose those results to the remaining TRACE modules.

The fundamental principle is:

AI/LLMs may interpret quantitative results, but they must never invent or calculate the authoritative KPI values.

Module 1 must answer:

What data exists?
Which datasets are available?
What does each field represent?
What data type does each field have?
What is the grain of each source?
Which fields are dimensions?
Which fields are measures?
Which fields represent dates/time?
How should datasets be related?
How fresh is each source?
Is the data complete?
Is the data valid?
Are there duplicates?
Are there referential-integrity problems?
What is the current KPI value?
What was the KPI historically?
How is the KPI calculated?
Which dimensions can the KPI be broken down by?
Which business drivers are associated with the KPI?
Where did the KPI value come from?
What source, columns, filters and calculations produced it?
2. Module 1 Position in TRACE

The complete reasoning chain remains:

                    MODULE 1
       Governed Data + KPI Foundation
                     │
                     ▼
                    MODULE 2
            Signal / Anomaly Engine
                     │
                     ▼
                    MODULE 3
           Driver + Hypothesis Engine
                     │
                     ▼
                    MODULE 4
          Evidence + Retrieval / RAG
                     │
                     ▼
                    MODULE 5
       Intelligence / Story / Recommendation
                     │
                     ▼
                   DECISION
                     │
                     ▼
                    MODULE 6
          Governance + Audit + Feedback
                     │
                     ▼
                    MODULE 7
       Runtime / Refresh / Reliability

Module 1 is therefore the ground-truth quantitative layer.

3. Strict Module 1 Boundaries

Module 1 does contain:

1. User Data Onboarding
2. Business Data Model
3. Schema Inference
4. Column Mapping
5. Data Validation
6. Data Profiling
7. Data Quality
8. Dataset Normalization
9. Synthetic Data Generation
10. SQLite Schema
11. KPI Semantic Contract
12. KPI Calculation Engine
13. Historical KPI Layer
14. Data Freshness
15. KPI Lineage
16. KPI API

Module 1 does not contain:

❌ RAG
❌ Embeddings
❌ Vector retrieval
❌ LLM narrative generation
❌ Chatbot reasoning
❌ Recommendations
❌ Causal inference
❌ Hypothesis ranking
❌ Final anomaly detection
❌ Evidence scoring

These remain responsibilities of downstream modules.

4. Current Project Architecture

The existing project uses:

Next.js
TypeScript
SQLite
sqlite
sqlite3
Recharts
React Flow
Jest
Supertest
tsx

For the current prototype:

Do not introduce Express, Prisma, PostgreSQL, another backend, or another database for Module 1.

Use the existing:

Next.js + SQLite

architecture.

SQLite remains the structured-data source of truth for the hackathon prototype.

The architecture must nevertheless keep the database layer abstract enough that production deployment can later move to PostgreSQL.

5. Current Problems Module 1 Must Fix
5.1 KPI data is JSON-based

Current architecture:

src/server/data/generateData.js
        ↓
public/data/kpis.json
        ↓
src/server/kpi/index.ts

This is not acceptable as the authoritative architecture.

Replace with:

Business Data
     ↓
SQLite
     ↓
KPI Calculation
     ↓
KPI Semantic Contract
     ↓
KPI API
5.2 AOV calculation is mathematically incorrect

The previous implementation effectively performs:

SUM(AOV)

This is incorrect.

AOV must be:

AOV = Total Revenue / Total Orders

Never calculate AOV by summing AOV values.

Never calculate an overall AOV by simply averaging regional AOVs.

The semantic layer must explicitly define this.

5.3 Existing anomaly logic is unreliable

The previous implementation compared an aggregate KPI against individual region/product observations when calculating a z-score.

That is statistically inappropriate.

Module 1 must not implement sophisticated anomaly detection.

Module 1 provides:

clean historical KPI observations
deterministic KPI calculations
dimensions
data quality
freshness
lineage

Module 2 performs:

signal detection
anomaly detection
materiality
statistical significance
prioritization
5.4 Existing evidence retrieval is fake

Current evidence search uses random vectors.

That is not a real RAG system.

Module 1 must not implement RAG.

Module 4 owns:

embeddings
retrieval
reranking
evidence scoring

Module 1 only provides structured business data and metadata.

5.5 Decisions have duplicate storage

Current architecture contains:

public/data/decisions.json

and:

db/trace.db

This creates multiple sources of truth.

Final architecture:

SQLite = authoritative persistence

Remove the duplicate JSON persistence path for decisions.

This is the major addition required to turn TRACE into an actual BusinessIntelligence.ai product.

The user must be able to select:

Add Data Source

The initial prototype should support:

The architecture should support future:

   ↓

   ↓

   ↓
Create/update KPI definitions
   ↓
Dataset ready for analysis
7. Supported Dataset Types

Module 1 must distinguish between structured business data and unstructured contextual data.

7.1 Structured Data

Used for quantitative analysis:

CSV

REST API

Module 1 owns the structured-data onboarding.

7.2 Unstructured Data

Examples:

PDF
DOCX
TXT
Reports
Customer complaints
Support tickets
Internal documents

These will eventually be handled by Module 4.

Module 1 should only register their existence and basic metadata if necessary.

Do not duplicate Module 4's document/RAG architecture.

9. Schema Inference

Module 1 must automatically inspect uploaded datasets.

For example:

Date          → DATE/TIMESTAMP
Revenue       → NUMERIC MEASURE
Orders        → INTEGER MEASURE
Region        → CATEGORICAL DIMENSION
Product       → CATEGORICAL DIMENSION
Channel       → CATEGORICAL DIMENSION

The system should infer:

column name
data type
semantic type
nullable/non-nullable
unique ratio
cardinality
likely dimension/measure
date/time field
possible identifier
possible foreign key
possible KPI field

Example:

{
  "column": "net_sales",
  "physicalType": "number",
  "semanticType": "measure",
  "role": "revenue_candidate",
  "nullable": false
}
10. Column Mapping

Different businesses will use different column names.

Company A:

net_sales
territory
txn_date

Company B:

revenue
region
sales_date

Company C:

sales_amount
market
date

TRACE should map them to canonical concepts:

net_sales
revenue
sales_amount
       ↓
    Revenue

territory
region
market
       ↓
    Region

txn_date
sales_date
date
       ↓
      Date

The system should suggest mappings automatically but allow the user to correct them.

11. Canonical Business Data Model

After mapping, arbitrary user schemas should be converted into a governed canonical representation.

USER DATA
   ↓
Schema Mapper
   ↓
Canonical Business Model
   ↓
KPI Semantic Layer
   ↓
Analysis Engine

This ensures that Modules 2–6 do not need to understand every company's proprietary column naming convention.

12. Dataset Grain Detection

Every source must have a known grain.

Examples:

Sales:
one row = one transaction

Marketing:
one row = date × region × channel × campaign

Operations:
one row = date × region × product

User uploads must similarly be classified.

Example:

Detected grain:

Date × Region × Product

If grain cannot be reliably determined, the user must be warned.

13. Dataset Relationship Detection

Module 1 should identify possible relationships between datasets.

Example:

sales.region
       ↕
marketing.region

sales.date
       ↕
marketing.date

sales.product_id
       ↕
operations.product_id

This enables:

Sales
+
Marketing
+
Operations

to become a coherent analytical environment.

This is essential for the problem statement's requirement to reconcile heterogeneous sources with different grains and refresh cadences.

14. User Confirmation

TRACE should not silently make dangerous semantic assumptions.

After inference, show:

Dataset: sales.csv

Detected:

Date        → transaction_date
Revenue     → net_revenue
Region      → region
Product     → product_id
Channel     → channel
Orders      → order_id

Detected grain:
Transaction

[Confirm Mapping]
[Edit Mapping]

The user confirms before the dataset becomes authoritative.

Example:

Dataset Health

Columns:            14

Missing values:     1.2%
Duplicate rows:     0.2%
Invalid dates:      0
Invalid revenue:    12

Quality Score:      97.8%

This becomes important for lineage and auditability.

The problem statement explicitly requires different refresh cadences.

Represent:

ERP
→ hourly

Marketing
→ daily

Customer reviews

Finance
→ daily

Manual upload
→ user controlled

Future database/API sources can have automatic refresh.

23. Stale Data Handling

If data becomes stale:

Data stale
    ↓
Module 6 notified
    ↓
Confidence affected
    ↓
User sees warning

Example:

Marketing data is 31 hours old. Marketing-related conclusions may be incomplete.

Never silently treat stale data as current.

29. Controlled Business Scenario

The synthetic dataset must contain a known multi-factor KPI movement.

Recommended scenario:

August — North Region Revenue Decline
North Region
     ↓
Inventory availability deteriorates
     ↓
Stockouts increase
     ↓
Premium product sales decrease
     ↓
Product mix deteriorates
     ↓
AOV decreases
     ↓
Revenue decreases

At the same time:

North marketing campaign
        ↓
Conversion rate decreases
        ↓
Orders decrease

Additional factor:

Discounting increases
        ↓
AOV decreases further

Potential drivers:

Stockouts
Product Mix
Discounting
Conversion
Orders
AOV
Marketing
30. Ground Truth

Create a controlled ground-truth definition for evaluation.

Example:

August Revenue Decline — North

Primary drivers:
- AOV decline
- Product mix deterioration
- Stockouts

Secondary drivers:
- Conversion decline
- Marketing performance

Expected confidence:
Medium/High

The ground truth must never be exposed to the LLM.

It exists only for evaluation.

The purpose is to measure whether TRACE identifies the known drivers.

31. KPI Semantic Contract

Create:

src/server/kpi/definitions.ts

This is the authoritative definition of every KPI.

Every KPI definition must contain:

id
name
description
formula
sourceTables
sourceColumns
grain
dimensions
refreshFrequency
materialityThreshold
owner
drivers
accessLevel

For user-created KPIs, these fields must be populated from:

user mapping
+
detected schema
+
user confirmation
32. Required KPIs

Implement at least five.

KPI 1 — Revenue
Revenue = SUM(net_revenue)

Source:

sales_transactions

Dimensions:

region
product
channel
date
KPI 2 — Orders
Orders = COUNT(DISTINCT order_id)

Source:

sales_transactions
KPI 3 — AOV
AOV = Revenue / Orders

Important:

Never calculate AOV by summing AOV values or simply averaging dimensional AOVs.

KPI 4 — Conversion Rate
Conversion Rate =
SUM(conversions) / SUM(sessions)

Source:

marketing_daily
KPI 5 — Marketing ROI
Marketing ROI =
SUM(attributed_revenue) / SUM(spend)

Source:

marketing_daily
33. User-Defined KPI Support

This is important for making BusinessIntelligence.ai genuinely useful beyond the demo.

A business user should eventually be able to define:

New KPI

For example:

Customer Retention Rate

Formula:

returning_customers / total_customers

TRACE stores the definition:

id
name
description
formula
source
columns
grain
dimensions
drivers
threshold
owner
access level

The formula must be deterministic.

The LLM may help the user express what they want, but the final formula must be validated and represented explicitly.

37. Deterministic Calculation Rules

Examples:

Revenue
SUM(net_revenue)
Orders
COUNT(DISTINCT order_id)
AOV
SUM(net_revenue)
----------------
COUNT(DISTINCT order_id)
Conversion
SUM(conversions)
----------------
SUM(sessions)
Marketing ROI
SUM(attributed_revenue)
-----------------------
SUM(spend)

No LLM is allowed to perform these calculations.

45. New Data Onboarding APIs

To support actual user uploads, add:

POST /api/data/upload

Creates an onboarding job.

GET /api/data/datasets

Lists available datasets.

GET /api/data/datasets/:id

Returns dataset metadata.

GET /api/data/datasets/:id/preview

Returns preview rows.

GET /api/data/datasets/:id/schema

Returns inferred schema.

POST /api/data/datasets/:id/mapping

Saves user-approved column mappings.

POST /api/data/datasets/:id/validate

Runs validation.

POST /api/data/datasets/:id/activate

Makes the dataset available for KPI analysis.

These are new Module 1 APIs, not replacements for the existing KPI APIs.

46. Large Upload Jobs

        ↓

        ↓

        ↓

        ↓

The existing implementation plan already establishes this background-job pattern for large ingestion.

This allows:

to plug into TRACE without rewriting the intelligence engine. The existing implementation plan already specifies this abstraction.

51. Database Abstraction

Current:

Module
 ↓
SQLite

Desired:

Module
 ↓
Repository
 ↓
Database

Example:

src/server/repositories/
├── datasetRepository.ts
├── kpiRepository.ts
├── decisionRepository.ts
├── auditRepository.ts
├── analysisRepository.ts
└── feedbackRepository.ts

This keeps SQLite replaceable later.

The existing implementation plan already requires this abstraction for scalability.

52. SQLite Limitation

SQLite is appropriate for:

local prototype
single-user demo
hackathon

It is not ideal for:

hundreds of concurrent writes
distributed deployment
multi-instance backend

Therefore:

Do not prematurely rewrite the prototype to PostgreSQL.

Keep database access behind repositories.

53. Synthetic Data Generation

The existing generator:

src/server/data/generateData.js

must remain.

It should:

Generate 12–18 months
Generate sales transactions
Generate marketing data
Generate operations data
Inject controlled business events
Create ground truth
Seed SQLite

But synthetic data must now implement the same canonical schema and KPI contracts as uploaded data.

This is critical.

Synthetic
     │
     ├──────────────┐
     │              │

     │              │
     └──────┬───────┘
            ▼
       Same M1 Layer
54. Synthetic Data Is Not the Product

The final architecture must explicitly state:

Synthetic data is a controlled prototype source used to demonstrate TRACE's intelligence pipeline. It is not the definition of the BusinessIntelligence.ai product.

The actual product is:

Bring your business data
        ↓
TRACE understands it
        ↓
TRACE defines/uses KPIs
        ↓
TRACE detects important movements
        ↓
TRACE investigates why
        ↓
TRACE finds evidence
        ↓
TRACE explains
        ↓
TRACE recommends

authoritative.

59. Module 1 → Module 4

Governed sales data supports the Orders hypothesis

Module 5:

Revenue declined primarily alongside a substantial decline in Orders...

This separation must remain.

60. Module 1 → Chatbot

The chatbot must never directly invent numbers.

For:

"What was revenue last month?"

the chatbot should query:

KPI API

For:

"Why did revenue fall?"

the chatbot should initiate:

Module 2 → Module 3 → Module 4 → Module 5

Module 1 remains the quantitative source.

61. Module 1 → RAG

RAG may consume:

KPI metadata
KPI values
dimensions
lineage
freshness
quality
source metadata

But Module 1 does not perform retrieval.

62. Role-Based Access

The KPI semantic contract contains:

accessLevel

This must be respected when exposing datasets and KPI data.

Future access levels can include:

public
team
manager
finance
executive
restricted

The architecture should support row-, column- and domain-level restrictions required by the problem statement.

63. Sparse History

Module 1 must expose historical coverage.

Example:

Revenue
History: 18 months

New Product X
History: 2 months

The engine must not fabricate historical observations.

Instead:

Sparse history
      ↓
Module 2
      ↓
Reduced statistical confidence

This supports the challenge's required newly launched/sparse-history scenario.

64. Multi-Source Data

The prototype must demonstrate:

Sales
Marketing
Operations

with different:

grains
refresh cadences
historical coverage

This directly satisfies the minimum prototype expectation of 3–5 connected KPIs across 2–3 sources with different grains or refresh cadences.

65. Data Refresh Architecture

Operational refresh remains primarily Module 7's responsibility.

The separation is:

M1 = What does the data mean?

M7 = How/when does data arrive and refresh?

Therefore:

M1
schema
mapping
grain
semantics
quality
KPI
lineage
M7
scheduler
refresh
retry
job management
source health
freshness monitoring
runtime

The existing plan already defines the scheduler, registry, connector interface and freshness architecture in this manner.

TRACE — MODULE 2 IMPLEMENTATION PLAN
Signal / Anomaly Detection, Materiality, Prioritisation & Investigation Trigger Engine

Status: 🟡 Partially implemented — substantial code exists, but Module 2 must be hardened and made source-agnostic before it can be considered complete.

Module: Module 2

Primary responsibility:

Determine whether a KPI movement is meaningful enough to warrant investigation, quantify the strength and confidence of that signal, and pass a structured investigation context to Module 3.

0. Status Legend

Use these throughout this plan.

Status	Meaning
🟢 IMPLEMENTED	Present and verified in the current repository
🟡 PARTIAL / VERIFY	Some implementation exists, but correctness/completeness must be verified or fixed
🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT	Required by the plan but not currently confirmed/implemented
🔵 DEPENDENCY READY	Required upstream/downstream interface exists or is defined

Important: I am deliberately not calling something "implemented" merely because the old checklist contains it. The current repository code was checked where possible.

1. Purpose of Module 2

Module 1 answers:

"What is the number?"

Module 2 answers:

"Is the movement meaningful?"

Module 3 answers:

"Why did it happen?"

Therefore:

MODULE 1
Governed KPI Foundation
        │
        │ KPI + history + metadata + quality + freshness + lineage
        ▼
MODULE 2
Signal / Anomaly Engine
        │
        │ meaningful KPI signal
        ▼
MODULE 3
Driver + Hypothesis Engine

The central design principle remains:

Revenue ↓ 8%
        ≠
Anomaly = TRUE

Instead:

Revenue ↓ 8%
      │
      ├── Historical baseline
      ├── Historical volatility
      ├── Seasonal behaviour
      ├── Statistical deviation
      ├── Business materiality
      ├── Data quality
      ├── Data freshness
      ├── Historical coverage
      └── Data source / dataset context
              │
              ▼
        Signal Strength
              │
              ▼
        Signal Confidence
              │
              ▼
       Priority + Status
              │
              ▼
         Investigation?

This is exactly the conceptual boundary established by the current implementation plan.

2. BusinessIntelligence.ai Product Context

Module 2 must not assume that the data comes from TRACE's synthetic dataset.

The same Signal Engine must work for:

Synthetic data
       │
       ├───────────────┐
       │               │
User CSV upload   User XLSX upload
       │               │
       └───────┬───────┘
               │
          Module 1
               │
      Governed KPI layer
               │
               ▼
          Module 2

Therefore Module 2 must be dataset-agnostic.

It should not contain:

if metric === "revenue"
   use sales_transactions

as its fundamental architecture.

Instead:

KPI Definition
      │
      ├── source
      ├── formula
      ├── grain
      ├── dimensions
      └── historical observations
              │
              ▼
       Generic Signal Engine

This is necessary because the product vision is a business-intelligence platform that can analyze a company's own data, while synthetic data is only the controlled prototype mechanism. The current plan explicitly makes this distinction.

3. What Module 2 Owns

Module 2 owns:

├── Historical baseline
├── Period-over-period analysis
├── Statistical deviation
├── Robust statistical deviation
├── Historical volatility
├── Seasonality adjustment
├── Business materiality
├── Data-quality adjustment
├── Freshness adjustment
├── Historical-coverage confidence
├── Signal-strength calculation
├── Signal prioritisation
├── Signal status
├── Signal confidence
├── Signal reason codes
├── Multi-dimensional signal detection
└── Signal APIs

This responsibility list is explicitly present in the current plan.

4. What Module 2 Does NOT Own

Module 2 must not perform:

❌ LLM narrative generation
❌ RAG
❌ document retrieval
❌ semantic evidence retrieval
❌ root-cause determination
❌ causal claims
❌ final recommendations
❌ decision memory
❌ human decision recording

For example:

Correct
Revenue declined 17.8%.

Statistical significance:
HIGH

Business materiality:
HIGH

Signal:
CRITICAL

Confidence:
94%
Incorrect
Revenue declined because of stockouts.

That requires Module 3 and subsequent evidence validation.

This boundary is important because the Round 2 brief explicitly says the LLM should not be treated as the source of quantitative truth and teams should distinguish SQL, statistics, rules, ML, causal inference, retrieval and LLM responsibilities.

5. Inputs From Module 1

Module 2 must consume:

Current KPI
Historical KPI
KPI metadata
KPI semantic definition
Dimensions
Data quality
Freshness
Historical coverage
Dataset/source metadata
Dataset version
Lineage
Status
🟢 Current KPI API dependency — READY
🟢 Historical KPI input — READY
🟢 KPI metadata input — READY
🟢 Quality input — READY
🟢 Freshness input — READY
🟡 Dataset/source/version context — must be propagated from the revised Module 1

The current Signal Engine already imports computeKPI, getKPIHistoryBatched, KPI definitions, quality and freshness services.

6. New Requirement: Uploaded Dataset Context

Because Module 1 now supports user-uploaded data, Module 2 must receive additional context.

Every signal should know:

datasetId
datasetVersionId
sourceType
sourceName
grain
dateField
dimensions

Example:

{
  "datasetId": "sales-001",
  "datasetVersionId": "sales-001-v4",
  "sourceType": "xlsx",
  "sourceName": "Company Sales Upload",
  "grain": "transaction"
}

This is 🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT / integrate.

The current KPISignal interface has metric, period, dimensions and telemetry, but does not contain explicit dataset/version/source lineage fields.

7. User-Uploaded Data Must Follow the Same Signal Pipeline

For synthetic data:

Synthetic Dataset
      ↓
KPI
      ↓
Module 2

For user data:

sales.xlsx
      ↓
Module 1
      ↓
KPI
      ↓
Module 2

The Signal Engine must not know whether the KPI came from:

sales_transactions

or:

uploaded_sales_v4

It should only consume the governed KPI contract.

Status

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT completely

8. Signal Engine Architecture

The intended architecture is:

                    MODULE 1
                       │
          ┌────────────┴────────────┐
          │                         │
    Current KPI              Historical KPI
          │                         │
          └────────────┬────────────┘
                       ▼
               ┌──────────────┐
               │ Signal Engine│
               └──────┬───────┘
                      │
       ┌──────────────┼──────────────┐
       ▼              ▼              ▼
   Baseline       Statistics     Materiality
       │              │              │
       └──────────────┼──────────────┘
                      ▼
                 Seasonality
                      │
                      ▼
               Quality/Freshness
                      │
                      ▼
                  Confidence
                      │
                      ▼
                Signal Strength
                      │
                      ▼
                   Priority
                      │
                      ▼
                    Status
                      │
                      ▼
                 Reason Codes
                      │
                      ▼
                  KPISignal

This architecture is already defined and substantially represented in the repository.

Status

🟢 ARCHITECTURE IMPLEMENTED

9. Signal Input Validation

Before analysis:

Validate metric
Validate period
Validate filters
Validate KPI availability
Validate historical coverage
Validate numeric observations
Validate source freshness
Validate data quality

Reject:

unsupported metric
invalid period
invalid dimension
invalid filter
missing KPI

The current /api/signals route already validates the required period and handles unsupported metrics/unknown dimensions.

Status

🟡 PARTIAL / VERIFY

10. Period-over-Period Analysis

Module 2 must calculate:

Current KPI
Previous KPI
Absolute change
Percentage change

Formula:

Absolute Change =
Current − Previous
Percentage Change =
(Current − Previous)
------------------- × 100
      Previous

Example:

Current = ₹760M
Previous = ₹925M

Change = −₹165M

Change % = −17.8%

The current implementation already obtains value, previousValue, and changePct.

Status

🟡 IMPLEMENTATION EXISTS / VERIFY

11. MoM and YoY

Module 2 should distinguish:

MoM
Current month vs previous month
YoY
Current month vs same month previous year

For user data, the comparison must be based on the actual dataset's available time grain.

For example:

Monthly data → MoM / YoY
Weekly data → WoW / YoY
Daily data → DoD / WoD / YoY

The current implementation has YoY/seasonality logic but should be generalized to dataset/KPI time grain.

Status

🟡 PARTIAL

12. Historical Baseline

Create/use:

src/server/signal/baseline.ts

Current repository already has this file.

The baseline should calculate:

Mean
Median
Standard deviation
MAD
Percentiles
History length

Current implementation calculates all of these.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

But:

The baseline implementation must be reviewed for the distinction between baseline periods and current observation.

Do not accidentally include the current observation in the historical baseline if the statistical design assumes an out-of-sample comparison.

Status

🟡 REVIEW REQUIRED

13. Mean

Calculate:

μ = Σx / n

Used as one baseline measure.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

14. Median

Calculate:

Median(history)

Useful for skewed business data.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

15. Standard Deviation

Use sample standard deviation:

s = √[Σ(x − x̄)² / (n − 1)]

The current baseline implementation uses n - 1.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

16. MAD — Median Absolute Deviation

Calculate:

MAD = median(|xi − median(x)|)

Robust against extreme observations.

Current implementation has MAD.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

17. Percentiles

Provide:

P10
P25
P50
P75
P90

Current implementation calculates these.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

18. Historical Volatility

Module 2 must calculate historical volatility.

For example:

Volatility =
standard deviation of historical KPI changes

or, depending on KPI type:

coefficient of variation =
standard deviation / mean

The method must be explicitly defined rather than left ambiguous.

High volatility should reduce anomaly confidence.

Status

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT AS A DEDICATED COMPONENT

Important:

The plan specifies volatility.ts, but the current repository search does not show a dedicated src/server/signal/volatility.ts. The current scoring configuration contains a highVolatility confidence penalty, but that alone does not constitute volatility calculation.

19. Z-Score

Formula:

Z = (Current − Mean) / Standard Deviation

Interpretation:

|Z| < 1       → normal-ish
1–2           → mild deviation
2–3           → significant deviation
>3            → strong deviation

Current repository calculates Z-score.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

20. Robust Z-Score

Formula:

Robust Z =
0.6745 × (Current − Median) / MAD

Current implementation uses this formula.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

21. Zero-MAD Fallback

Problem:

MAD = 0

This occurs frequently for:

low-variance KPIs
newly launched KPIs
sparse data
repeated identical values

The engine must not divide by zero.

Required fallback:

MAD > 0
    ↓
Robust Z-score

MAD = 0
    ↓
Fallback to standard deviation
    ↓
If stdDev = 0
    ↓
No statistical anomaly
Current status

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT CORRECTLY

The current implementation only calculates robust Z when MAD > 0. It does not establish a proper explicit zero-MAD fallback.

22. Statistical Significance

Keep:

Statistical Significance

separate from:

Business Materiality

Possible classification:

NONE
LOW
MEDIUM
HIGH

Current code uses Z-score thresholds:

low    = 1
medium = 2
high   = 3

These thresholds are currently configurable.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

Required improvement

The current code uses the standard zScoreThresholds for the maximum of Z and robust Z. There is also a separate robustZScoreThresholds configuration, but the anomaly code does not actually use that separate configuration.

Therefore:

🟡 FIX REQUIRED

23. Anomaly Detection

An anomaly should require more than simply:

change > 5%

The current logic effectively requires:

statistical significance
AND
change > 5%

or:

high statistical deviation

The existing code implements this.

Problem

The 5% threshold is embedded directly in anomaly.ts.

That violates the requirement:

No scattered magic thresholds.

Required change

Move all such thresholds to:

signal/config.ts

Example:

anomaly: {
  minimumAbsoluteChangePct: 5
}
Status

🟡 PARTIAL / FIX REQUIRED

24. Business Materiality

Business materiality answers:

"Even if this movement is statistically unusual, is it important to the business?"

Example:

Revenue ↓ 2%

could be:

Statistically significant = YES
Business materiality = LOW

while:

Revenue ↓ 18%

could be:

Statistically significant = HIGH
Business materiality = HIGH

The two must remain independent.

The current repository has materiality.ts and configurable materiality thresholds.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

25. Materiality Thresholds

Current configuration contains examples:

Revenue:
absolute = 500,000
relative = 3%

Orders:
absolute = 500
relative = 5%

AOV:
relative = 3%

Conversion:
absolute = 2
relative = 10%

Marketing ROI:
absolute = 0.2
relative = 15%

These should remain configurable.

Major required change

For uploaded user datasets, fixed absolute thresholds can be meaningless.

Example:

Company A revenue = ₹100M
Company B revenue = ₹100B

A ₹500K threshold means completely different things.

Therefore Module 2 needs:

KPI-specific thresholds
+
dataset/business-scale-aware thresholds
+
relative thresholds

For the prototype:

semantic contract threshold

should be preferred over global hard-coded assumptions.

Status

🟡 PARTIAL

26. Seasonality

Module 2 must determine whether the movement is expected because of seasonal behavior.

Example:

December revenue ↓ vs November

might not be anomalous.

Compare:

December 2026
vs
December 2025

rather than only:

December 2026
vs
November 2026

Current repository has seasonality.ts.

The current signal pipeline calls seasonality detection.

Status

🟡 IMPLEMENTED / VERIFY

27. Seasonality History Requirement

Current configuration:

seasonality.enabled = true
minHistoryMonths = 12

This is sensible for monthly YoY comparison.

But user-uploaded data may contain:

3 months
6 months
9 months

In those cases:

seasonality unavailable

must be explicitly represented.

Do not fabricate seasonality.

Status

🟡 PARTIAL

28. Critical Current Seasonality/Data Bug

The current implementation uses:

historyMap.get(period) || 0
historyMap.get(prevYearPeriod) || 0

This means missing observations can become zero.

That is dangerous.

For example:

Actual data:
January = ₹10M
February = missing

must not become:

February = ₹0

because:

missing ≠ zero

This is particularly important for user-uploaded datasets.

Required fix

Use:

undefined / null

for missing observations.

Only use zero when the business semantics explicitly define zero.

Status

🔴 YET TO FIX

29. Data Quality Adjustment

Data quality must affect confidence.

Inputs:

completeness
null rate
duplicate rate
validity
referential integrity

Current scoring consumes a DataQualityResult.

Status

🟡 IMPLEMENTED / VERIFY

The scoring layer currently reduces its quality score based on warning and critical states.

30. Freshness Adjustment

Fresh data:

confidence unaffected

Stale data:

confidence reduced

Critical stale data:

larger confidence penalty

Current scoring contains this behavior.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

But it must be verified against the revised Module 1 freshness model for user-uploaded data.

31. Historical Coverage

Confidence must depend on the amount of history.

Current scoring:

≥12 periods → 1.0
≥6 periods  → 0.8
≥3 periods  → 0.5
<3          → 0.2

This is already implemented in scoring.ts.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

But these values should be moved entirely into configuration.

32. Sparse History

Required behavior:

New KPI
   ↓
Only 2–5 historical observations
   ↓
Do not pretend statistical confidence is high

Example:

KPI:
New Product Revenue

History:
3 months

Signal:
Potential movement

Confidence:
LOW

Status:
WATCH

Reason:
SPARSE_HISTORY

Current configuration contains:

sparseHistory.minPeriods = 6
sparseHistory.lowConfidenceThreshold = 0.4

and the scoring logic applies a sparse-history penalty.

However, the implementation plan explicitly says sparse-history handling is not yet confirmed complete.

Status

🔴 YET TO CONFIRM / COMPLETE

33. Low-Confidence Scenario

Required prototype scenario:

Evidence/history insufficient
        ↓
Confidence < threshold
        ↓
Do not overstate anomaly
        ↓
WATCH / ABSTAIN / REQUEST MORE DATA

For example:

Revenue appears unusual, but only four historical observations are available. TRACE cannot reliably determine whether this is an anomaly.

Status

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT / VERIFY

This is explicitly required by the Round 2 prototype expectations.

34. Normal / Noise Scenario

TRACE must demonstrate that it does not flag everything.

Example:

Revenue:
+1.8%

Z-score:
0.42

Materiality:
Low

Signal:
NORMAL

Confidence:
High

No investigation should be triggered.

Status

🔴 YET TO CONFIRM COMPLETE

Required:

Revenue:
−17.8%

−3.8

Robust Z:
−4.0

Materiality:
High

Signal:
Critical

Status:
Urgent

High

The current repository can produce this general signal structure.

But the complete scenario must be deterministically tested.

Status

🟡 PARTIAL / TEST REQUIRED

36. Multi-Dimensional Signals

Module 2 must support:

Revenue
      │
      ├── Overall
      ├── Region
      │     ├── North
      │     ├── South
      │     ├── East
      │     └── West
      │
      ├── Product
      └── Channel

Current API supports:

region
product

as filters.

rather than only:

37. Generic Dimension Support

New architecture:

dimensions: Record<string, string>

Example:

{
  "region": "North",
  "product": "Premium",
  "channel": "Online"
}

For another company:

{
  "country": "Germany",
  "business_unit": "Industrial",
  "customer_segment": "Enterprise"
}

Module 2 should not have hard-coded knowledge of only three dimensions.

Status

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT FULLY

38. Signal Strength

Signal strength combines:

Statistical evidence
+
Business materiality
+
Historical confidence
+
Data quality
+
Seasonality

Current weights:

Statistical        0.35
Materiality        0.30
Historical         0.15
Data Quality       0.10
Seasonality        0.10

These are present in the current configuration.

Formula:

Signal Strength =
0.35 × Statistical Score
+
0.30 × Materiality Score
+
0.15 × Historical Confidence
+
0.10 × Data Quality Score
+
0.10 × Seasonality Score
Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

39. Problem in Current Signal Strength

The current scoring.ts calls:

calculateSeasonalityScore({ adjusted: false })

rather than passing the actual seasonality result.

Therefore the seasonality component of signal strength is not actually using the detected seasonality.

This must be fixed.

Status

🔴 BUG — MUST FIX

40. Priority

Required:

LOW
MEDIUM
HIGH
CRITICAL

Current configuration:

low      = 0.25
medium   = 0.50
high     = 0.75
critical = 1.00

Current implementation has a problematic mapping:

≥0.75 → high
≥0.50 → high

meaning the configured medium priority is not actually returned.

Required behavior
0.00–0.24 → low
0.25–0.49 → medium
0.50–0.74 → high
0.75–1.00 → critical

or another explicitly documented mapping.

Status

🟡 IMPLEMENTED BUT INCORRECT / FIX REQUIRED

41. Status

Required:

NORMAL
WATCH
INVESTIGATE
URGENT

Current repository implements these statuses.

Current thresholds:

watch       = 0.35
investigate = 0.55
urgent      = 0.85
Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

But status should also account for:

confidence

A signal with:

strength = 0.9
confidence = 0.25

should not necessarily be shown as a high-confidence urgent issue.

Status

🟡 IMPROVEMENT REQUIRED

42. Confidence

Confidence must represent:

"How much should TRACE trust its own signal?"

It should incorporate:

Historical coverage
Data quality
Freshness
Volatility
Seasonality availability
Statistical robustness

Current scoring begins confidence from signal strength and applies penalties.

Status

🟡 IMPLEMENTED / REWORK REQUIRED

43. Confidence Must Not Equal Signal Strength

This distinction is critical.

Example:

Signal Strength = 0.91
Confidence = 0.42

means:

The movement looks important, but TRACE has insufficient evidence to be highly confident.

This is exactly the sort of uncertainty handling expected by the Round 2 problem.

44. High Volatility Penalty

If:

historical volatility = high

then:

confidence ↓

because large movements are normal for that KPI.

Status

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT

The configuration contains highVolatility, but there is no confirmed dedicated volatility computation feeding the penalty.

45. Reason Codes

Signals should return machine-readable reasons.

Examples:

LARGE_MOM_CHANGE
LARGE_YOY_CHANGE
HIGH_Z_SCORE
HIGH_ROBUST_Z_SCORE
HIGH_MATERIALITY
SEASONAL_PATTERN
SPARSE_HISTORY
STALE_DATA
LOW_COMPLETENESS
HIGH_VOLATILITY
NEW_KPI

The current type definition already contains these reason codes.

Status

🟢 DEFINED

But

The scoring implementation currently does not generate all of them.

For example, it explicitly adds LARGE_MOM_CHANGE and HIGH_MATERIALITY, but does not comprehensively populate all available codes.

Status

🟡 PARTIAL / COMPLETE REQUIRED

46. Reason Code Requirements

Every signal should explain:

Why was it flagged?

Example:

{
  "reasonCodes": [
    "LARGE_MOM_CHANGE",
    "HIGH_Z_SCORE",
    "HIGH_MATERIALITY",
    "HISTORICAL_COVERAGE_GOOD"
  ]
}

The current example structure already exists in the plan.

For user data:

STALE_DATA
SPARSE_HISTORY
LOW_COMPLETENESS

must be automatically generated where applicable.

🟡 INTERFACE DEFINED

59. Multi-Factor Business Movement

The Round 2 requirement is not merely:

Revenue ↓

It expects a multi-factor movement with known/simulated underlying drivers.

Module 2's role is:

Revenue ↓
      │
      ├── unusual?
      ├── material?
      ├── seasonal?
      ├── statistically significant?
      └── worthy of investigation?

Module 3 then determines:

Orders
AOV
Product Mix
Stockouts
Marketing
Conversion

as candidate explanations.

60. Controlled Prototype Scenario

The existing scenario should remain:

North Region Revenue
        ↓
Revenue decreases
        ↓
AOV decreases
        ↓
Orders decrease
        ↓
Product mix deteriorates
        ↓
Stockouts increase
        ↓
Marketing conversion weakens

Module 2 must detect:

Revenue movement = meaningful

without claiming:

Stockouts caused revenue decline

That remains Module 3/4 territory.

61. Source Heterogeneity

The Round 2 brief requires:

3–5 connected KPIs across 2–3 data sources with different grains or refresh cadences.

Module 2 must therefore not assume:

all KPIs have same grain
all sources refresh simultaneously
all history lengths are equal

Example:

Sales
grain: transaction
refresh: hourly

Marketing
grain: date × campaign × region
refresh: daily

Operations
grain: date × region × product
refresh: every 6 hours

Module 2 must use Module 1's governed KPI output to normalize these differences.

62. Missing Data

Critical rule:

missing ≠ zero

If:

Marketing data missing for August

do not calculate:

marketing spend = 0

Instead:

Marketing KPI:
Unavailable

Confidence:
Reduced

Reason:
MISSING_DATA
Status

🔴 YET TO FULLY IMPLEMENT

This is especially important for uploaded user datasets.

63. Contradictory Data

If:

Sales source says:
Revenue = ₹100M

Finance source says:
Revenue = ₹85M

Module 2 must not silently choose one.

Module 1 should reconcile or flag source conflicts.

Module 2 should receive:

data-quality warning

and reduce confidence if necessary.

Status

🟡 ARCHITECTURE REQUIRED

new_product_sales.xlsx

2 months of history

LOW

SPARSE_HISTORY

Recommendation:

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT / TEST

68. Negative/Zero Baseline Scenario

unavailable

where appropriate.

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT / VERIFY

69. Outlier-Robustness

The engine should compare:

Standard Z-score

and:

Robust Z-score

Example:

Z = 1.2
Robust Z = 3.4

This indicates:

The standard baseline may be distorted by extreme historical observations.

The signal should use the robust evidence appropriately.

Status

🟢 PARTIAL IMPLEMENTATION

The two scores are calculated, but the scoring/threshold architecture needs correction and explicit testing.

70. Signal Explanation

Module 2 can generate a technical explanation, not an LLM narrative.

Example:

Revenue decreased 17.8% month-over-month.
The movement is 3.8 standard deviations below
the six-month baseline and exceeds the configured
business materiality threshold.

This is deterministic.

Module 5 later converts the verified intelligence into persona-specific natural language.

Status

🟡 PARTIAL

71. No LLM in Module 2

Do not add:

GPT
Claude
Gemini
Ollama
LLM

to the core signal calculation.

The signal must be reproducible:

same input
     ↓
same signal

This is also required for defensibility.

72. Determinism

Given:

KPI
history
configuration
quality
freshness

the output must always be identical.

Example:

Signal Strength = 0.817

not:

0.81 one time
0.87 another time

unless the underlying data/configuration changed.

Status

🔴 TESTING YET TO CONFIRM

85. Required Test Matrix
Scenario	Expected
Small normal movement	NORMAL
Moderate movement	WATCH
Large statistically significant movement	INVESTIGATE
Very large material movement	URGENT
Sparse history	Low confidence
No history	No statistical anomaly
Missing data	Never converted to zero
Constant series	No NaN/Infinity
Zero MAD	Fallback
High volatility	Confidence reduced
Stale data	Confidence reduced
Poor completeness	Confidence reduced
Strong seasonality	Avoid false anomaly
User uploaded CSV	Works

  ↓

A === B

for all analytical values.

for every UI component.

91. Most Important Changes Compared With the Existing Module 2

The old Module 2 was essentially:

KPI
 ↓
Anomaly
 ↓
Signal

The revised BusinessIntelligence.ai Module 2 must become:

ANY GOVERNED BUSINESS DATA
          ↓
      KPI + History
          ↓
   ┌──────────────────┐
   │ Signal Engine    │
   │                  │
   │ Baseline         │
   │ Statistics       │
   │ Robust Statistics│
   │ Volatility       │
   │ Seasonality      │
   │ Materiality      │
   │ Quality          │
   │ Freshness        │
   │ History Coverage │
   └────────┬─────────┘
            ↓
       Confidence
            ↓
      Signal Strength
            ↓
     Priority + Status
            ↓
       Reason Codes
            ↓
      Investigation

And the critical product-level change is:

              USER
               │
        ┌──────┴───────┐
        │              │
   Synthetic       Own Data
     Data         CSV/XLSX/JSON
        │              │
        └──────┬───────┘
               ▼
          MODULE 1
               ▼
          MODULE 2
               ▼
          MODULE 3
               ▼
          MODULE 4
               ▼
          MODULE 5
               ▼
            ACTION

That is the correct direction for the BusinessIntelligence.ai requirement rather than building a signal detector that only works against the existing demo dataset.

The Round 2 brief specifically calls for material KPI detection, heterogeneous sources, semantic contracts, sparse-history handling, role/security considerations, evidence of freshness/method/contribution/confidence/lineage, and a clear LLM vs non-LLM boundary.

Bottom line: Module 2 already has a real foundation in the repository — especially the signal pipeline, baseline statistics, anomaly calculation, scoring, caching and three APIs. The work now is not to throw it away, but to correct the analytical bugs, complete the missing statistical components, make the engine generic for Module 1's user-uploaded datasets, implement the low-confidence/sparse-history behavior, and then lock it down with deterministic tests.







































TRACE — MODULE 3 UPDATED IMPLEMENTATION PLAN
Driver Decomposition, Driver Analysis & Hypothesis Engine
BusinessIntelligence.ai Edition

Module: Module 3
Primary question:

"What could explain this KPI movement?"

Module 2 determines:

Is the movement meaningful?

Module 3 determines:

Which measurable factors could explain it, how strongly are they associated with the movement, when do they move relative to it, where is the relationship consistent, and what hypotheses deserve investigation?

Module 4 then asks:

What evidence supports or contradicts those hypotheses?

This separation is central to the existing architecture.

1. Status Legend
Status	Meaning
🟢 IMPLEMENTED	Exists in the current repository and is part of the current architecture
🟡 PARTIAL / VERIFY	Exists, but requires correction, integration verification, or hardening
🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT	Required by the revised plan but not currently complete
🔵 DOWNSTREAM / DEPENDENCY	Belongs to another module but Module 3 must provide/consume the interface
2. Module 3 Position in BusinessIntelligence.ai

The complete architecture becomes:

USER / ENTERPRISE DATA
        │
        ├── CSV
        ├── XLSX
        ├── JSON
        ├── structured systems
        └── synthetic/demo data
                 │
                 ▼
            MODULE 1
       GOVERNED KPI FOUNDATION
                 │
                 │ KPI + metadata + history
                 ▼
            MODULE 2
        SIGNAL / ANOMALY ENGINE
                 │
                 │ meaningful KPI movement
                 ▼
            MODULE 3
      DRIVER + HYPOTHESIS ENGINE
                 │
                 │ candidate explanations
                 ▼
            MODULE 4
        EVIDENCE / RAG ENGINE
                 │
                 │ verified evidence
                 ▼
            MODULE 5
    NARRATIVE + RECOMMENDATION
                 │
                 ▼
             DECISION
                 │
                 ▼
            MODULE 6
      GOVERNANCE / AUDIT / LEARNING

The Round 2 BusinessIntelligence.ai brief explicitly requires detection, reconciliation, driver ranking, evidence-backed narratives, uncertainty/abstention, recommendations, feedback learning, and realistic security/cost/latency/scalability.

Module 3 receives:

+

+

+
Alternative hypotheses

Caveats

This is already reflected in the current DriverAnalysis contract.

4. What Module 3 Must Never Become

Module 3 is not:

❌ A generic LLM chatbot
❌ A root-cause hallucination engine
❌ A document retrieval system
❌ A recommendation engine
❌ A narrative generator
❌ A causal inference engine by default
❌ A replacement for Module 4
❌ A replacement for Module 5

It is:

Quantitative driver analysis
+
Hypothesis generation
+
Hypothesis ranking
+
Evidence request generation
5. User-Uploaded Data Requirement

This is the major change from the earlier implementation plan.

Module 3 must work with:

Synthetic data
       +
User-uploaded CSV
       +
User-uploaded XLSX
       +
User-uploaded JSON

through Module 1.

The flow must be:

User uploads sales.xlsx
        ↓

        ↓

        ↓

        ↓

        ↓

        ↓
Module 3

Module 3 must not directly parse arbitrary uploaded files.

Status

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT AS A COMPLETE DATASET-AWARE FLOW

from accidentally being analysed using:

Company B history

11. Driver Discovery

Do not ask an LLM:

"What caused revenue to fall?"

Instead:

Revenue
  ↓
Driver graph
  ↓
Candidate drivers
  ↓
Check data availability
  ↓
Calculate measurable relationships

Only drivers with valid data should proceed.

The existing plan explicitly requires controlled candidate drivers rather than arbitrary LLM-generated hypotheses.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

12. Data Availability Gate

Before analysing a driver:

Does the dataset contain the required variable?
        │
        ├── YES → analyse
        │
        └── NO → unsupported / insufficient data

Example:

Revenue
      │
      ├── Orders → available
      ├── AOV → available
      ├── Stockouts → available
      ├── Marketing → unavailable
      └── Competition → unavailable

The system should not invent missing drivers.

Status

🟡 IMPLEMENTED / VERIFY

The current types explicitly support unsupportedDriver and unsupportedDrivers.

13. Segment Contribution vs Driver Contribution

These must remain separate.

Segment contribution

Answers:

WHERE did the movement occur?

Example:

North = 70%
South = 10%
East = -5%
West = 25%
Driver contribution

Answers:

WHAT measurable factor is associated with the movement?

Example:

AOV = 54%
Orders = 31%
Stockouts = 11%

This distinction is explicitly required by the current plan.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

14. Total KPI Change

Module 3 must calculate:

currentValue
previousValue
totalChange
totalChangePct

Example:

Previous Revenue = ₹925M
Current Revenue = ₹760M

Total change = −₹165M
Change % = −17.8%
Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

17. Signed Contribution

Use:

signedContributionPct =
    driverChange / totalChange × 100

This may be:

>100%

or:

negative

when factors offset each other.

Example:

Total = -₹1M

North = -₹700K → 70%
East = +₹500K → -50%

Do not clamp this.

The current types.ts explicitly documents this distinction.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

18. Magnitude Contribution

Use:

magnitudeContributionPct =
|driverChange|
-----------------------
Σ |all driver changes|
× 100

This always lies between:

0–100%

and answers:

How large was this component relative to total movement?

The current implementation explicitly supports both signed and magnitude contributions.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

19. Exact Algebraic Decomposition

When a valid equation exists, use it.

Example:

Revenue = Orders × AOV

Therefore:

Revenue
├── Orders
└── AOV

This is fundamentally different from merely observing correlation.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

20. Revenue Decomposition

For:

Previous:
Orders = 100,000
AOV = ₹100

Current:
Orders = 96,000
AOV = ₹95

Revenue moves:

₹10M → ₹9.12M

Module 3 should identify:

Orders effect
+
AOV effect

with interaction allocated transparently.

The existing plan specifies this exact decomposition.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

21. Shapley-Style Decomposition

For:

Revenue = Orders × AOV

calculate:

f00 = f(oldOrders, oldAOV)
f10 = f(newOrders, oldAOV)
f01 = f(oldOrders, newAOV)
f11 = f(newOrders, newAOV)

Then:

Orders contribution =
[(f10 − f00) + (f11 − f01)] / 2

and:

AOV contribution =
[(f01 − f00) + (f11 − f10)] / 2

Guarantee:

Orders contribution
+
AOV contribution
=
Total revenue change

subject to floating-point precision.

This is already implemented according to the current plan and type contracts.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

22. Reconciliation Check

Every exact decomposition must verify:

Σ contributions ≈ total KPI change

with:

reconciliationError

and:

reconciles = true / false

The current DriverContribution and ShapleyDecomposition contracts explicitly expose this.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

23. Exact vs Statistical Contribution

Every driver contribution must be labelled:

exact
statistical
insufficient_data
not_exactly_decomposable

Example:

Revenue → Orders
exact

Revenue → Marketing
statistical

Revenue → Competition
insufficient_data

This is one of the strongest parts of the current architecture.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

24. Critical Mathematical Rule

Never claim:

Orders = Sessions × Conversion

unless the underlying governed KPI definition actually guarantees this relationship.

The current plan explicitly says Sessions × Conversion must not be treated as an exact decomposition of Orders because they come from different governed sources.

Instead:

Sessions ↔ Orders
Conversion ↔ Orders

can be analysed statistically.

Status

🟢 RULE IMPLEMENTED

25. AOV Decomposition

AOV:

AOV = Revenue / Orders

Exact decomposition is valid only when:

previousOrders > 0
AND
currentOrders > 0

Otherwise:

insufficient_data

The current implementation explicitly handles this.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

26. Marketing ROI Decomposition

Marketing ROI requires special treatment because:

ROI = Revenue attributable to marketing / Marketing Spend

It should not be treated as a simple additive KPI.

The current driver implementation has explicit Marketing ROI handling.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

Required

Test it against uploaded datasets where:

marketing spend = 0

or:

attributed revenue unavailable

🟡 EDGE-CASE TESTING REQUIRED

27. Statistical Driver Analysis

driver history
        +

28. Pearson Correlation

Calculate:

r =
Cov(X,Y)
---------
σX σY

Calculate it on aligned movement series, not blindly on raw levels.

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

29. Spearman Correlation

Use rank correlation for monotonic but non-linear relationships.

Example:

Driver increases
KPI consistently decreases

even if the relationship is not linear.

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

n = 2

as reliable statistical evidence.

must be configurable.

  "sampleSize": 3,
  "insufficientData": true

The current type contract explicitly supports this.

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

🔴 EXPAND TEST COVERAGE

31. Association Strength

Classify:

none

strong

based on configurable thresholds.

33. Correlation Is Not Causation

Consistent with

🟢 POLICY IMPLEMENTED

34. Temporal Alignment

A driver moving before the KPI is generally more informative than a driver moving after it.

Example:

Stockouts
    ↓
Revenue

is more temporally plausible than:

Revenue
    ↓
Stockouts

The current architecture has:

temporal.ts

and TemporalAlignment.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

35. Lag Analysis

Evaluate:

lag = −N ... +N

and determine:

bestLag
lagCorrelation
lagDirection

Current semantics:

L > 0 → driver leads KPI
L = 0 → contemporaneous
L < 0 → driver follows KPI

This is explicitly defined in the current contract.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

36. Temporal Score

Convert temporal alignment into:

0–1

with:

driver leads KPI → stronger
contemporaneous → moderate
driver follows KPI → penalised
Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

Required

🟡 VERIFY AGAINST REAL DATA

37. Segment Consistency

A driver should not be considered strong merely because it correlates at aggregate level.

Example:

Overall:
Stockouts ↔ Revenue = strong

North:
strong

South:
strong

East:
weak

West:
strong

This provides:

segmentConsistency = 75%

The current implementation contains segment consistency and affected/unaffected segments.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

38. Affected Segments

Track:

affectedSegments

Example:

[
  {
    "segment": "North",
    "driverChangePct": -21,
    "kpiChangePct": -18
  }
]
Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

39. Unaffected Segments

Track segments where the KPI did not materially move.

This is important because:

driver changed everywhere

but:

KPI changed only in North

weakens a universal driver explanation.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

40. Counter-Segment Comparison

Compare:

Affected segments
vs
Unaffected segments

Example:

North:
Stockouts +35%
Revenue -18%

South:
Stockouts +2%
Revenue +1%

This strengthens the hypothesis.

The current contract includes CounterSegmentComparison.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

41. Contradiction Detection

A hypothesis should be weakened if:

Expected direction:
negative

Observed:
positive

Example:

Hypothesis:
Stockouts caused revenue decline

Observed:
Stockouts decreased 10%

This is contradictory evidence.

The current architecture includes contradiction.ts and Contradiction.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

42. Contradiction Types

Support:

association contradiction

with:

invalidates

The current contract explicitly defines these.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

43. Contradiction Must Be Significant

Do not call:

a contradiction merely because it is positive.

Contradiction logic should respect:

sample size
p-value
association strength

The current implementation notes specifically require significance for association-based contradiction.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

47. Score Breakdown

Every hypothesis should expose:

{
  "contribution": 0.81,
  "association": 0.93,
  "temporal": 0.88,
  "segmentConsistency": 0.91,
  "causalPlausibility": 0.75,
  "evidenceAvailability": 0.60,
  "contradictionPenalty": 0.05
}

This makes the score explainable.

The current contract supports scoreBreakdown.

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

48. Causal Plausibility

This does not mean causal inference.

It represents whether the relationship is:

business-mechanistically plausible

Example:

Stockouts → Revenue decline

is more plausible than:

Revenue decline → Weather sensor noise

Use:

business rules
semantic graph
domain knowledge

rather than an LLM's unsupported opinion.

Status

🟡 IMPLEMENTED / VERIFY DEFINITION

Orders

Inventory

50. Important Boundary With Module 4

inventory

Retrieve stockout records
Retrieve customer complaints
Retrieve fulfillment reports

Module 3 must not perform retrieval itself.

The current plan explicitly defines this boundary.

Status

🟢 ARCHITECTURE IMPLEMENTED

51. Evidence Requests

For every serious hypothesis, generate:

hypothesisId
metric

driver

filters
requiredEvidence

Example:

{
  "hypothesisId": "H1",
  "metric": "revenue",
  "period": "2026-08",
  "driver": "stockouts",
  "query": "North stockouts August",
  "filters": {
    "region": "North",
    "dateStart": "2026-08-01",
    "dateEnd": "2026-08-31"
  },
  "requiredEvidence": [
    "inventory",
    "stockout_records",
    "fulfillment"
  ]
}

The current contract already contains this structure.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

52. User-Uploaded Dataset Evidence Requests

New requirement:

For uploaded data:

datasetId
datasetVersionId

must be propagated into the evidence request context.

Otherwise Module 4 could retrieve evidence from the wrong dataset.

Status

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT

53. Hypothesis Status

Use:

strong_candidate
candidate
weak_candidate
insufficient_data

The current DriverHypothesis type already defines these.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

54. Confidence

Confidence should not simply equal:

score

Instead it should account for:

data availability
sample size
statistical significance
temporal quality
segment consistency
contradictions
evidence availability
Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

Required

🟡 CALIBRATION TESTING REQUIRED

55. Sparse History

Example:

New product
History = 3 months

The system should say:

H1: Product mix

Status:
insufficient_data

Confidence:
low

Caveat:
Only three historical periods are available.

It must not pretend:

H1 confidence = 92%
Status

🟡 SUPPORTED BY CONTRACT / TESTING REQUIRED

56. Missing Driver Data

Example:

Revenue ↓ 12%

Stockout history:
missing

Output:

Stockouts:
insufficient_data

not:

Stockouts:
0%
Status

🟢 ARCHITECTURE SUPPORTS THIS

Status

🟡 REQUIRE EDGE-CASE TEST

57. No Driver Data

If:

Revenue ↓ 12%

but all candidate driver data is missing:

status = insufficient_data

and:

evidenceRequests = [...]

rather than fabricating a cause.

Status

🔴 MUST EXPLICITLY TEST

58. Ambiguity Handling

If:

H1 = 0.73
H2 = 0.71

the engine must not say:

H1 is the root cause.

Instead:

AMBIGUOUS

1. Stockouts — 73%
2. Product mix — 71%

Additional evidence required.

The current plan explicitly requires this behavior.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

59. Ambiguity Margin

The difference between top candidates should be configurable.

Example:

ambiguityMargin = 0.05

Then:

H1 = 0.73
H2 = 0.71
difference = 0.02

→ ambiguous.

Status

🟢 CONFIGURATION IMPLEMENTED

Status

🟡 VERIFY APPLICATION

60. Alternative Hypotheses

Do not return only:

Top driver

Return:

Top driver
+
2–3 alternatives

Example:

1. Orders decline       84%
2. AOV decline          68%
3. Product mix          61%
4. Stockouts            56%

This directly supports BusinessIntelligence.ai's requirement for uncertainty and alternative explanations.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

61. Caveats

Every hypothesis may include:

caveats[]

Examples:

"Only six historical observations were available."

"Marketing data is refreshed daily while revenue is monthly."

"Correlation does not establish causality."

"Inventory data was unavailable."

Current type supports this.

Status

🟢 IMPLEMENTED

62. Multi-Source / Different-Grain Data

Round 2 specifically requires:

3–5 connected KPIs across 2–3 data sources with different grains or refresh cadences.

Example:

Sales
grain: transaction
refresh: hourly

Marketing
grain: campaign × day
refresh: daily

Operations
grain: product × region × day
refresh: every 6 hours

Module 3 must receive aligned KPI/driver series from Module 1 rather than assuming identical grains.

Status

🟡 PARTIAL / DEPENDENT ON MODULE 1

63. Temporal Normalisation

Before correlation:

transaction-level sales
        ↓
monthly KPI

campaign-level marketing
        ↓
monthly KPI

operations-level stockouts
        ↓
monthly KPI

Then:

2026-01
2026-02
...
2026-08

are aligned.

Module 3 should not correlate:

transaction rows

directly against:

monthly KPI
Status

🟡 DEPENDENT ON M1 / VERIFY

64. Refresh Cadence Awareness

A driver updated:

daily

should not automatically be interpreted as:

monthly causal movement

without aggregation/alignment.

Store:

source refresh cadence
KPI grain
driver grain
alignment method
Status

🔴 YET TO FULLY IMPLEMENT

For each KPI:

The Round 2 brief explicitly calls for a lightweight KPI/semantic contract covering these concepts.

🟡 DEPENDENCY ON MODULE 1

66. Role-Based Security

Module 3 must respect upstream entitlements.

Example:

Analyst
  → regional data

Regional Manager
  → regional + product data

Executive
  → aggregated company data

Do not allow:

Module 3
→ bypass Module 1 security
→ expose restricted rows

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT / MODULE 1 + GOVERNANCE DEPENDENCY

67. Row/Column/Domain Security

For sensitive datasets:

Row-level:
North only

Column-level:
revenue visible
customer_email hidden

Domain-level:
Marketing domain unavailable

Module 3 should receive only authorised governed data.

🔴 YET TO IMPLEMENT

69. Driver Provenance
